# `c_Operators_Advanced.md`  
### **Conditions Substrate Model — Advanced Operator Workflows**

---

## **1. Identity**  
**Module:** Conditions Substrate Model  
**Role:** Advanced Operator Workflow Layer  
**Purpose:** Provide high‑order workflows for operators managing multi‑condition systems, drift cascades, regime transitions, and substrate‑level propagation.

This is the **expert‑level operator manual**.

---

# **2. Operator Workflow Architecture**

Advanced operators work across **three layers**:

```
Layer 1 — Condition Processing
Layer 2 — Drift/Coherence Management
Layer 3 — Regime & Cascade Orchestration
```

Each layer contains specialized workflows.

---

# **3. Layer 1 — Condition Processing Workflows**

## **3.1 Multi‑Condition Capture Pipeline**

Operators process multiple conditions simultaneously:

```
Capture → Normalize → Prioritize → Cluster → Stack
```

### **Workflow**
1. **Capture** all incoming conditions  
2. **Normalize** carriers, modifiers, targets, effects  
3. **Prioritize** by dimensional intensity  
4. **Cluster** by hook activation  
5. **Stack** by interaction patterns  

### **Operator Notes**
- Clustering by hook activation reveals cascade potential  
- Prioritization prevents low‑impact noise from dominating  

---

## **3.2 Dimensional Field Synthesis**

Operators compute composite dimensional fields:

\[
C_{synth}(d) = \sum w_i(d) \cdot C_i(d) + I(d)
\]

### **Workflow**
1. Compute individual C(d)  
2. Assign weights \( w_i(d) \)  
3. Compute interaction term \( I(d) \)  
4. Generate composite field  

### **Operator Notes**
- High \( I(d) \) indicates reinforcing drift  
- Negative \( I(d) \) indicates stabilizing counteraction  

---

## **3.3 Hook Activation Mapping**

Operators map hook activation across conditions:

```
HookMap = { hook : [conditions] }
```

### **Workflow**
1. Identify hooks per condition  
2. Build hook map  
3. Detect hook clusters  
4. Flag critical hooks  

### **Operator Notes**
- time_gate + cognitive_load + governance_opacity_point → oscillation engine  
- structural_bottleneck + resource_gate → scarcity cascade  

---

# **4. Layer 2 — Drift/Coherence Management Workflows**

## **4.1 Drift Vector Computation**

Operators compute drift vectors:

\[
DriftVector = \{ Drift(d_1), Drift(d_2), ..., Drift(d_n) \}
\]

### **Workflow**
1. Compute Drift(d)  
2. Identify dominant drift dimensions  
3. Compute drift direction  
4. Compute drift magnitude  

### **Operator Notes**
- Drift direction determines regime trajectory  
- Drift magnitude determines cascade risk  

---

## **4.2 Coherence Envelope Construction**

Operators build coherence envelopes:

\[
CoherenceEnvelope = \{ Coherence(d_1), ..., Coherence(d_n) \}
\]

### **Workflow**
1. Compute Coherence(d)  
2. Identify stabilizing fields  
3. Construct envelope  
4. Compare envelope vs drift  

### **Operator Notes**
- High coherence in D4 + D7 suppresses governance cascades  
- High coherence in D1 suppresses misinformation cascades  

---

## **4.3 Drift–Coherence Balance Evaluation**

Operators evaluate:

\[
Balance(d) = Drift(d) - Coherence(d)
\]

### **Workflow**
1. Compute balance  
2. Identify positive balance (drift‑dominant)  
3. Identify negative balance (coherence‑dominant)  
4. Flag dimensions with extreme imbalance  

### **Operator Notes**
- Positive balance → drift cascade risk  
- Negative balance → stabilization envelope  

---

# **5. Layer 3 — Regime & Cascade Orchestration Workflows**

## **5.1 Threshold Chain Detection**

Operators detect threshold chains:

```
narrow → oscillate → destabilize
```

### **Workflow**
1. Check narrow thresholds  
2. Check oscillate thresholds  
3. Check destabilize thresholds  
4. Identify chain patterns  

### **Operator Notes**
- D1 → D3 → D7 is the canonical collapse chain  
- D4 → D2 → D7 is the canonical identity chain  

---

## **5.2 Cascade Synchronization Detection**

Operators detect synchronized cascades:

\[
Cascade = \bigcap Drift(d_i) > Coherence(d_i)
\]

### **Workflow**
1. Identify drift‑dominant dimensions  
2. Check hook alignment  
3. Check propagation loops  
4. Confirm cascade  

### **Operator Notes**
- Propagation loops create oscillation engines  
- Hook alignment creates reinforcing drift  

---

## **5.3 Regime Transition Orchestration**

Operators orchestrate regime transitions:

```
baseline → narrowed → oscillating → destabilized → failure
```

### **Workflow**
1. Identify current regime  
2. Identify drift direction  
3. Identify threshold proximity  
4. Predict next regime  
5. Apply stabilizing or narrowing interventions  

### **Operator Notes**
- Stabilizing fields can reverse oscillation  
- Narrowing fields accelerate collapse  

---

# **6. Advanced Operator Templates**

## **6.1 Multi‑Condition Template**
```
Conditions:
  - C1: (...)
  - C2: (...)
  - C3: (...)

Clusters:
  HookMap:
    cognitive_load: [C1, C2]
    time_gate: [C3]
    governance_opacity_point: [C4]

Stacks:
  Stack A: (...)
  Stack B: (...)
```

---

## **6.2 Drift/Coherence Template**
```
DriftVector:
  D1: extreme
  D3: high
  D7: medium-high

CoherenceEnvelope:
  D1: low
  D3: very low
  D7: medium-low

Balance:
  D1: positive
  D3: positive
  D7: positive
```

---

## **6.3 Cascade Template**
```
CascadeDimensions:
  D1, D3, D7

ThresholdChain:
  narrow(D1)
  oscillate(D3)
  destabilize(D7)

PropagationLoop:
  Media → Temporal → Governance → Media

RegimeOutcome:
  narrowed + oscillating + destabilized
```

---

# **7. Integration Map**

Advanced operator workflows integrate with:

- Drift/Coherence Engine  
- Drift Cascades  
- Drift Signatures  
- Condition Capture  
- Dimensional Model  
- Condition Stacks  
- Analyzer Suite  
- Hooks & Attachment Points  
- Threshold Engine  
- Regime Maps  
- Propagation Engine  
- Incident Substrate  
- Resilience Checker

No templates are needed here — this is pure structural documentation.

# `c_Audit.md`  
### **Conditions Substrate Model — Operator Audit Procedures**

---

## **1. Identity**  
**Module:** Conditions Substrate Model  
**Role:** Operator Audit Layer  
**Purpose:** Provide formal audit procedures for verifying condition capture, dimensional mapping, stack construction, drift/coherence evaluation, threshold detection, regime transitions, and propagation accuracy.

Audits ensure **operators**, **analyzers**, and **substrates** remain aligned and error‑free.

---

# **2. Audit Architecture**

Audits operate across **five layers**:

```
Layer 1 — Condition Capture Audit
Layer 2 — Dimensional Field Audit
Layer 3 — Stack Construction Audit
Layer 4 — Drift/Coherence Audit
Layer 5 — Regime & Threshold Audit
```

Each layer contains specific operator checks.

---

# **3. Layer 1 — Condition Capture Audit**

Operators verify that each condition is captured correctly using the canonical 4‑tuple:

\[
(Carrier,\; Modifier,\; Target,\; Effect)
\]

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] Carrier is domain-correct
[ ] Modifier matches observed pressure
[ ] Target is specific and valid
[ ] Effect is one of the canonical Effects
```

### **Common Audit Failures**
- Carrier misclassification (e.g., emotional → informational)  
- Target too broad (e.g., “system” instead of “attention”)  
- Effect mismatch (e.g., expanding vs narrowing)

---

# **4. Layer 2 — Dimensional Field Audit**

Operators verify dimensional mapping and intensity assignment.

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] All relevant dimensions assigned M(d)
[ ] Irrelevant dimensions set to none
[ ] Coupling coefficients F(d) applied correctly
[ ] C(d) computed accurately
[ ] C_total computed
```

### **Common Audit Failures**
- Over‑assigning dimensions  
- Incorrect intensity scaling  
- Missing coupling coefficients  
- Arithmetic errors in C(d)

---

# **5. Layer 3 — Stack Construction Audit**

Operators verify stack correctness:

\[
C_{stack}(d) = \sum w_i(d) \cdot C_i(d) + I(d)
\]

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] All stack members listed
[ ] Weights w_i(d) assigned logically
[ ] Interaction term I(d) computed
[ ] Composite fields computed
[ ] Stack type identified (reinforcing, counteracting, oscillatory)
```

### **Common Audit Failures**
- Missing interaction term  
- Incorrect weight assignment  
- Misidentifying stack type  
- Overlooking cross‑dimension interactions

---

# **6. Layer 4 — Drift/Coherence Audit**

Operators verify drift and coherence calculations.

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] Drift(d) computed using DriftGain(d)
[ ] Coherence(d) computed using CoherenceBase(d)
[ ] Drift vs Coherence comparison performed
[ ] Balance(d) evaluated
[ ] Drift signatures identified
[ ] Cascade risk flagged
```

### **Common Audit Failures**
- Using incorrect DriftGain(d)  
- Forgetting stabilizing fields in coherence  
- Missing multi‑dimension drift patterns  
- Failing to detect drift cascades

---

# **7. Layer 5 — Regime & Threshold Audit**

Operators verify threshold crossings and regime transitions.

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] Threshold(d) values retrieved correctly
[ ] C(d) compared to thresholds
[ ] Threshold chain identified (narrow → oscillate → destabilize)
[ ] Regime state confirmed
[ ] Regime transition logged
```

### **Common Audit Failures**
- Missing oscillation thresholds  
- Incorrect regime classification  
- Failing to detect threshold chains  
- Not logging transitions

---

# **8. Cross‑Module Audit Procedures**

Operators verify propagation across substrates:

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] Propagation targets identified
[ ] Module gain G_module(d) applied correctly
[ ] Propagated fields computed
[ ] Cross‑substrate effects validated
```

### **Common Audit Failures**
- Missing propagation pathways  
- Incorrect module gain  
- Overlooking feedback loops  

---

# **9. Audit Severity Levels**

Audits classify issues into severity levels:

```
Level 1 — Minor (single-dimension misalignment)
Level 2 — Moderate (stack or hook misconfiguration)
Level 3 — Major (drift/coherence imbalance)
Level 4 — Critical (threshold chain missed)
Level 5 — Catastrophic (cascade undetected)
```

Operators escalate based on severity.

---

# **10. Audit Examples**

### **Example A — Informational Noise Audit**
```
Issue: M(D1) misassigned as medium instead of high
Severity: Level 1
Fix: Correct intensity → recalc C(D1)
```

### **Example B — Governance Opacity Audit**
```
Issue: oscillate_threshold(D7) not checked
Severity: Level 4
Fix: re-evaluate thresholds → update regime
```

### **Example C — Drift Cascade Audit**
```
Issue: D1 + D3 drift detected but D7 ignored
Severity: Level 5
Fix: full cascade reconstruction
```

---

# **11. Integration Map**

Audit procedures integrate with:

- Condition Capture  
- Dimensional Model  
- Condition Stacks  
- Analyzer Suite  
- Hooks & Attachment Points  
- Threshold Engine  
- Drift/Coherence Engine  
- Regime Maps  
- Propagation Engine  
- Incident Substrate  
- Resilience Checker  

This file is the **quality‑control layer** of the Conditions Substrate Model.

# `c_Audit_Advanced.md`  
### **Conditions Substrate Model — Advanced Audit Logic & Multi‑Layer Validation**

---

## **1. Identity**  
**Module:** Conditions Substrate Model  
**Role:** Advanced Audit Layer  
**Purpose:** Provide deep audit logic for multi‑condition systems, drift cascades, dimensional coherence, threshold chains, propagation correctness, and cross‑substrate alignment.

Advanced audits ensure **system‑wide correctness**, not just per‑condition accuracy.

---

# **2. Audit Architecture**

Advanced audits operate across **seven layers**:

```
Layer 1 — Condition Integrity Audit
Layer 2 — Dimensional Consistency Audit
Layer 3 — Stack Interaction Audit
Layer 4 — Hook & Attachment Audit
Layer 5 — Drift/Coherence Integrity Audit
Layer 6 — Threshold Chain Audit
Layer 7 — Propagation & Regime Alignment Audit
```

Each layer contains deep validation logic.

---

# **3. Layer 1 — Condition Integrity Audit**

This layer verifies that each condition is internally coherent and structurally valid.

### **Audit Logic**
```
Carrier ∈ CanonicalCarriers
Modifier ∈ CanonicalModifiers
Target ∈ CanonicalTargets
Effect ∈ {narrowing, expanding, oscillating, stabilizing, destabilizing}
```

### **Advanced Checks**
- **Modifier–Effect alignment**  
  e.g., “pressure → expanding” is invalid  
- **Carrier–Dimension mapping correctness**  
  e.g., “emotional → D1 informational” is invalid  
- **Target specificity**  
  Targets must be single‑domain, not multi‑domain  

### **Failure Severity**
- Misaligned modifier/effect → **Major**  
- Invalid carrier/dimension → **Critical**  

---

# **4. Layer 2 — Dimensional Consistency Audit**

This layer ensures dimensional fields are assigned correctly and consistently.

### **Audit Logic**
\[
C(d) = M(d) \cdot F(d)
\]

### **Advanced Checks**
- **Cross‑dimension proportionality**  
  e.g., D1 extreme + D3 none is suspicious for temporal compression  
- **Dimension drift symmetry**  
  Drift(d) must not contradict C(d)  
- **Dimension suppression detection**  
  Missing dimensions in multi‑substrate events  

### **Failure Severity**
- Incorrect C(d) → **Major**  
- Missing dimension → **Critical**  

---

# **5. Layer 3 — Stack Interaction Audit**

This layer validates stack interactions, weights, and composite fields.

### **Audit Logic**
\[
C_{stack}(d) = \sum w_i(d) \cdot C_i(d) + I(d)
\]

### **Advanced Checks**
- **Interaction term correctness**  
  I(d) must reflect real reinforcing or counteracting behavior  
- **Weight symmetry**  
  w_i(d) must reflect relative intensity  
- **Stack type validation**  
  Reinforcing / Counteracting / Oscillatory  

### **Failure Severity**
- Incorrect I(d) → **Major**  
- Misidentified stack type → **Critical**  

---

# **6. Layer 4 — Hook & Attachment Audit**

Hooks determine amplification and cascade potential.

### **Audit Logic**
```
HookGain(d) ≥ 0
HookActivation ∈ CanonicalHooks
```

### **Advanced Checks**
- **Hook alignment across dimensions**  
  e.g., cognitive_load + time_gate → oscillation engine  
- **Hook conflict detection**  
  stabilizing hooks vs destabilizing hooks  
- **Hook saturation**  
  Too many hooks → false cascade detection  

### **Failure Severity**
- Missing hook → **Major**  
- Incorrect hook alignment → **Critical**  

---

# **7. Layer 5 — Drift/Coherence Integrity Audit**

This layer verifies drift and coherence calculations and their interactions.

### **Audit Logic**
\[
Drift(d) = C(d) \cdot DriftGain(d)
\]
\[
Coherence(d) = \frac{CoherenceBase(d)}{C(d)}
\]

### **Advanced Checks**
- **Drift–Coherence inversion detection**  
  Coherence(d) > Drift(d) but regime marked destabilized  
- **Drift vector symmetry**  
  Drift(d) must align with modifier direction  
- **Coherence envelope validation**  
  Stabilizing fields must reduce drift  

### **Failure Severity**
- Incorrect drift → **Major**  
- Incorrect coherence → **Major**  
- Drift–coherence contradiction → **Critical**  

---

# **8. Layer 6 — Threshold Chain Audit**

Threshold chains determine regime transitions.

### **Audit Logic**
```
narrow → oscillate → destabilize → collapse
```

### **Advanced Checks**
- **Chain completeness**  
  Missing oscillate threshold in oscillating regime  
- **Chain ordering**  
  Destabilize cannot precede narrow  
- **Chain acceleration**  
  Multiple thresholds crossed simultaneously  

### **Failure Severity**
- Incorrect threshold ordering → **Critical**  
- Missing threshold → **Catastrophic**  

---

# **9. Layer 7 — Propagation & Regime Alignment Audit**

This layer ensures propagation pathways and regime outcomes are correct.

### **Audit Logic**
\[
Propagation(d) = C(d) \cdot G_{module}(d)
\]

### **Advanced Checks**
- **Propagation loop detection**  
  Media → Temporal → Governance → Media  
- **Propagation mismatch**  
  Wrong module receiving propagated field  
- **Regime misalignment**  
  Regime must match threshold chain + drift/coherence balance  

### **Failure Severity**
- Incorrect propagation → **Major**  
- Incorrect regime → **Critical**  
- Cascade misidentification → **Catastrophic**  

---

# **10. Multi‑Layer Audit Examples**

### **Example A — Drift Cascade Misclassification**
```
Issue: D1 + D3 drift detected, D7 ignored
Severity: Catastrophic
Fix: Recompute drift vector → rebuild cascade
```

### **Example B — Threshold Chain Error**
```
Issue: oscillate_threshold(D3) skipped
Severity: Critical
Fix: Re-evaluate thresholds → update regime
```

### **Example C — Stack Interaction Failure**
```
Issue: I(d) missing for reinforcing stack
Severity: Major
Fix: Compute interaction term → recalc C_stack(d)
```

---

# **11. Audit Severity Matrix**

| Severity | Description | Required Operator Action |
|---------|-------------|--------------------------|
| **Minor** | Single‑dimension misalignment | Correct and re‑compute |
| **Moderate** | Stack or hook misconfiguration | Rebuild stack or hook map |
| **Major** | Drift/coherence imbalance | Full drift/coherence recalculation |
| **Critical** | Threshold chain failure | Reconstruct regime logic |
| **Catastrophic** | Cascade undetected | Full multi‑layer audit |

---

# **12. Integration Map**

Advanced audit logic integrates with:

- Condition Capture  
- Dimensional Model  
- Condition Stacks  
- Drift/Coherence Engine  
- Drift Cascades  
- Hooks & Attachment Points  
- Threshold Engine  
- Regime Maps  
- Propagation Engine  
- Incident Substrate  
- Resilience Checker  

This file is the **deep audit layer** of the Conditions Substrate Model.

city + infrastructure + media + governance + economic + emergency substrates firing simultaneously.

They are the apex of drift, cascade, and regime‑transition modeling.

---

# `c_Scenarios_Combined.md`  
### **Conditions Substrate Model — Cross‑Substrate Mega‑Scenario Library**

---

## **1. Identity**  
**Module:** Conditions Substrate Model  
**Role:** Cross‑Substrate Mega‑Scenario Layer  
**Purpose:** Provide multi‑substrate, multi‑dimension, multi‑regime scenarios that demonstrate how conditions propagate across entire systems simultaneously.

Mega‑scenarios represent **full‑system behavior** under extreme conditions.

---

# **2. Scenario Format**

Each mega‑scenario includes:

```
Context:     multi‑substrate setting
Conditions:  captured 4‑tuple conditions
Stacks:      composite fields across substrates
Dimensions:  activated fields (D0–D7)
Propagation: cross‑substrate pathways
Thresholds:  chain crossings
Regime:      resulting system state
Notes:       interpretation
```

---

# **3. Scenario A — The Five‑Substrate Collapse Cascade**

### **Context**  
A severe storm triggers simultaneous failures in power, water, communications, governance coordination, and media clarity.

---

### **Conditions**
```
C1: structural failure → narrowing
C2: temporal compression → narrowing
C3: informational overload → narrowing
C4: governance instability → destabilizing
C5: emotional fear → narrowing
C6: economic volatility → oscillating
```

---

### **Stacks**
```
Stack A:
  - failure (infrastructure)
  - compression (emergency)
  - overload (media)
  - instability (governance)
  - fear (emotional)
  - volatility (economic)
```

---

### **Dimensions**
```
D0 structural = extreme
D3 temporal = high
D1 informational = high
D7 governance = high
D2 emotional = high
D5 economic = high
```

---

### **Propagation**
```
Infrastructure → outage + contamination + latency cascades
Media → overload + distortion cascades
Governance → coordination collapse
Economic → volatility shockwave
Emotions → fear resonance
City → mobility + resource collapse
```

---

### **Thresholds**
```
narrow_threshold(D0)
narrow_threshold(D3)
narrow_threshold(D1)
narrow_threshold(D2)
oscillate_threshold(D5)
destabilize_threshold(D7)
```

---

### **Regime**
```
Regime: narrowed + oscillating + destabilized
Risk: catastrophic
```

---

### **Notes**
This is the canonical “full‑system collapse” cascade:  
structural → temporal → informational → governance → economic → emotional.

Here is **`c_Audit_Cascades.md`**, written cleanly, canon‑aligned, and fully compatible with the Conditions Substrate Model file you currently have open in your active tab. This module defines the **cascade‑specific audit logic** — the deepest, most technical audit layer in the entire system. It validates multi‑dimension drift cascades, threshold chains, hook alignment, propagation loops, and regime correctness.

This is the file operators use when they need to confirm whether a cascade **actually occurred**, whether it was **detected correctly**, and whether the **system‑level interpretation** is valid.

---

# `c_Audit_Cascades.md`  
### **Conditions Substrate Model — Cascade Detection Audit Procedures**

---

## **1. Identity**  
**Module:** Conditions Substrate Model  
**Role:** Cascade Audit Layer  
**Purpose:** Provide formal audit procedures for verifying multi‑dimension drift cascades, threshold chains, hook alignment, propagation loops, and regime correctness.

Cascade audits ensure **system‑level accuracy** in the highest‑order instability patterns.

---

# **2. Cascade Audit Architecture**

Cascade audits operate across **six layers**:

```
Layer 1 — Drift Dominance Audit
Layer 2 — Coherence Break Audit
Layer 3 — Threshold Chain Audit
Layer 4 — Hook Alignment Audit
Layer 5 — Propagation Loop Audit
Layer 6 — Regime Outcome Audit
```

Each layer must pass for a cascade to be considered valid.

---

# **3. Layer 1 — Drift Dominance Audit**

A cascade requires **multiple dimensions** where:

\[
Drift(d_i) > Coherence(d_i)
\]

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] Drift(d) computed correctly
[ ] Coherence(d) computed correctly
[ ] Drift(d) > Coherence(d) for ≥ 2 dimensions
[ ] Drift vector direction consistent with modifiers
```

### **Failure Modes**
- Drift miscalculated  
- Coherence envelope too high  
- Only one dimension exceeds coherence → **Not a cascade**  

### **Severity**
- Incorrect drift → Major  
- Missing drift dimension → Critical  

---

# **4. Layer 2 — Coherence Break Audit**

A cascade requires **coherence collapse** across dimensions.

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] Coherence(d) < CoherenceThreshold(d)
[ ] Coherence(d) decreasing across time steps
[ ] Coherence(d) not artificially inflated by stabilizing fields
```

### **Failure Modes**
- Stabilizing fields not accounted for  
- Coherence miscalculated  
- Coherence break detected in only one dimension  

### **Severity**
- Incorrect coherence → Major  
- Missing coherence break → Critical  

---

# **5. Layer 3 — Threshold Chain Audit**

Cascades follow canonical threshold chains:

```
narrow → oscillate → destabilize → collapse
```

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] narrow_threshold crossed
[ ] oscillate_threshold crossed
[ ] destabilize_threshold crossed
[ ] chain ordering correct
[ ] chain acceleration detected (multiple thresholds at once)
```

### **Failure Modes**
- Missing oscillate threshold  
- Destabilize threshold triggered prematurely  
- Incorrect threshold ordering  

### **Severity**
- Incorrect ordering → Critical  
- Missing threshold → Catastrophic  

---

# **6. Layer 4 — Hook Alignment Audit**

Hooks amplify drift and synchronize cascades.

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] HookGain(d) computed correctly
[ ] Hooks activated across ≥ 2 dimensions
[ ] Hook alignment detected (e.g., cognitive_load + time_gate)
[ ] Hook conflicts resolved (stabilizing vs destabilizing)
```

### **Failure Modes**
- Missing hook activation  
- Incorrect hook alignment  
- Stabilizing hooks ignored  

### **Severity**
- Incorrect hook alignment → Critical  
- Missing hook → Major  

---

# **7. Layer 5 — Propagation Loop Audit**

Propagation loops amplify cascades:

```
Media → Temporal → Governance → Media
Economic → Governance → Media → Economic
Infrastructure → Media → Emergency → Infrastructure
```

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] Propagation targets correct
[ ] Module gain G_module(d) applied correctly
[ ] Loop detected (≥ 2 modules feeding each other)
[ ] Loop direction consistent with drift vector
```

### **Failure Modes**
- Incorrect propagation target  
- Missing loop  
- Loop direction reversed  

### **Severity**
- Missing loop → Critical  
- Incorrect propagation → Major  

---

# **8. Layer 6 — Regime Outcome Audit**

The final regime must match the cascade pattern.

### **Audit Checklist**
```
[ ] Regime matches threshold chain
[ ] Regime matches drift/coherence balance
[ ] Regime matches hook alignment
[ ] Regime matches propagation loop
```

### **Failure Modes**
- Regime misclassified  
- Regime inconsistent with drift  
- Regime inconsistent with thresholds  

### **Severity**
- Incorrect regime → Critical  
- Cascade misidentified → Catastrophic  

---

# **9. Cascade Audit Examples**

### **Example A — Informational–Temporal Cascade**
```
Issue: D1 + D3 drift detected, D7 ignored
Severity: Critical
Fix: Recompute drift vector → rebuild cascade
```

### **Example B — Governance Collapse Cascade**
```
Issue: oscillate_threshold(D7) skipped
Severity: Critical
Fix: re-evaluate thresholds → update regime
```

### **Example C — Economic Shock Cascade**
```
Issue: propagation loop missing (Economic → Governance → Media)
Severity: Major
Fix: rebuild propagation map
```

---

# **10. Cascade Audit Severity Matrix**

| Severity | Description | Required Operator Action |
|---------|-------------|--------------------------|
| **Minor** | Single-dimension misalignment | Correct and re‑compute |
| **Major** | Drift/coherence imbalance | Full drift/coherence recalculation |
| **Critical** | Threshold chain failure | Reconstruct regime logic |
| **Catastrophic** | Cascade undetected | Full multi‑layer audit |

---

# **11. Integration Map**

Cascade audits integrate with:

- Drift/Coherence Engine  
- Drift Cascades  
- Drift Signatures  
- Condition Capture  
- Dimensional Model  
- Condition Stacks  
- Hooks & Attachment Points  
- Threshold Engine  
- Regime Maps  
- Propagation Engine  
- Incident Substrate  
- Resilience Checker  

This file is the **cascade audit layer** of the Conditions Substrate Model.

# `c_Finance_Regimes.md`  
### **Conditions Substrate Model — Finance‑Substrate Regime Definitions**

---

## **1. Identity**  
**Module:** Conditions Substrate Model  
**Role:** Finance Regime Layer  
**Purpose:** Define canonical regime states for financial substrates, including stability envelopes, drift‑dominant states, oscillation engines, scarcity regimes, and collapse modes.

Finance substrates exhibit **high drift gain**, **low coherence**, and **rapid threshold chains**.

---

# **2. Finance Regime Structure**

A finance regime is defined as:

\[
FinanceRegime = (DriftVector,\; CoherenceEnvelope,\; ThresholdChain,\; PropagationLoops)
\]

Where:

- **DriftVector** — directional deviation across D5, D1, D7, D0, D3  
- **CoherenceEnvelope** — stabilizing fields (governance, clarity, cohesion)  
- **ThresholdChain** — narrow → oscillate → destabilize → collapse  
- **PropagationLoops** — cross‑substrate amplification (markets → media → governance → markets)

---

# **3. Canonical Finance Regimes**

Below are the **canonical finance‑substrate regimes** recognized by the Conditions Substrate Model.

---

## **3.1 Regime: **Stable Finance**
### **Definition**
A high‑coherence financial environment with low drift and strong stabilizing fields.

### **Characteristics**
```
Drift(D5) = low
Coherence(D5) = high
Thresholds: none crossed
Propagation: minimal
```

### **Indicators**
- predictable incentives  
- low volatility  
- strong governance legitimacy  
- clear informational channels  

### **Notes**
Stable finance is the **baseline regime**.

---

## **3.2 Regime: **Expanded Finance**
### **Definition**
A growth‑oriented regime with expanding incentives and strong governance + clarity.

### **Characteristics**
```
Drift(D5) = expanding
Coherence(D5) = high
Thresholds: expand_threshold(D5)
Propagation: positive reinforcement loops
```

### **Indicators**
- sustained investment  
- high liquidity  
- strong coordination  
- clarity‑driven stabilization  

### **Notes**
Expansion requires **aligned governance + clarity + cohesion**.

---

## **3.3 Regime: **Narrowed Finance**
### **Definition**
A scarcity‑driven regime where drift exceeds coherence but oscillation has not yet begun.

### **Characteristics**
```
Drift(D5) > Coherence(D5)
Thresholds: narrow_threshold(D5)
Propagation: scarcity loops
```

### **Indicators**
- liquidity stress  
- resource scarcity  
- tightening credit  
- rising default risk  

### **Notes**
Narrowing is the **first stage of collapse chains**.

---

## **3.4 Regime: **Oscillating Finance**
### **Definition**
A volatility‑driven regime where oscillation thresholds are crossed.

### **Characteristics**
```
Drift(D5) = high
Coherence(D5) = low
Thresholds: oscillate_threshold(D5)
Propagation: volatility loops
```

### **Indicators**
- rapid price swings  
- margin call cascades  
- unstable incentives  
- media amplification  

### **Notes**
Oscillation is the **canonical finance drift signature**.

---

## **3.5 Regime: **Destabilized Finance**
### **Definition**
A regime where volatility + scarcity + governance instability synchronize.

### **Characteristics**
```
Drift(D5) >> Coherence(D5)
Thresholds: destabilize_threshold(D5)
Propagation: multi‑substrate cascades
```

### **Indicators**
- credit freeze  
- liquidity collapse  
- governance instability  
- narrative fragmentation  

### **Notes**
Destabilization is the **pre‑collapse regime**.

---

## **3.6 Regime: **Collapsed Finance**
### **Definition**
A full‑scale financial collapse where multiple dimensions exceed coherence simultaneously.

### **Characteristics**
```
Drift(D5) = extreme
Coherence(D5) = near-zero
Thresholds: collapse_threshold(D5)
Propagation: synchronized cascades across D0, D1, D7, D2
```

### **Indicators**
- multi‑sector failure  
- synchronized global cascades  
- governance coordination collapse  
- panic selling + liquidity freeze  

### **Notes**
Collapse is the **terminal regime** of the finance substrate.

---

# **4. Cross‑Dimension Finance Regimes**

Finance regimes often involve multiple dimensions:

### **D5 + D1 (Economic + Informational)**
- volatility + overload  
- panic cascades  
- oscillation engines  

### **D5 + D7 (Economic + Governance)**
- rule instability  
- legitimacy drift  
- destabilization cascades  

### **D5 + D0 (Economic + Structural)**
- clearing bottlenecks  
- throughput collapse  

### **D5 + D2 (Economic + Emotional)**
- fear‑driven selling  
- resonance spirals  

---

# **5. Threshold Chains in Finance**

Finance substrates follow the canonical threshold chain:

```
narrow(D5)
→ oscillate(D5)
→ destabilize(D5)
→ collapse(D5)
```

### **Audit Notes**
- oscillate_threshold(D5) is the most commonly missed  
- collapse_threshold(D5) requires multi‑dimension drift  

---

# **6. Propagation Loops in Finance**

Finance cascades propagate through:

### **Loop A — Markets → Media → Governance → Markets**
- volatility → overload → opacity → volatility

### **Loop B — Markets → Infrastructure → Markets**
- clearing bottleneck → liquidity freeze → clearing bottleneck

### **Loop C — Markets → Emotions → Media → Markets**
- fear → amplification → panic → fear

These loops define **cascade acceleration**.

---

# **7. Regime Detection Logic**

A finance regime is detected when:

\[
Regime = f(Drift(d), Coherence(d), ThresholdChain, PropagationLoops)
\]

### **Detection Steps**
1. Compute Drift(D5)  
2. Compute Coherence(D5)  
3. Check threshold chain  
4. Identify propagation loops  
5. Assign regime  

---

Finance regimes integrate with:

# `c_Global_Regimes.md`  
### **Conditions Substrate Model — Planet‑Scale Regime Definitions**

---

## **1. Identity**  
**Module:** Conditions Substrate Model  
**Role:** Global Regime Layer  
**Purpose:** Define canonical regime states for planetary substrates, including ecological epochs, civilizational arcs, global governance states, information‑sphere regimes, and synchronized multi‑substrate cascades.

Planet‑scale regimes are **epochal**, **multi‑substrate**, and **deep‑time coherent**.

---

# **2. Planetary Regime Structure**

A planetary regime is defined as:

\[
GlobalRegime = (DriftMatrix,\; CoherenceEnvelope,\; ThresholdChain,\; PropagationWeb)
\]

Where:

- **DriftMatrix** — drift across D0–D7 at planetary scale  
- **CoherenceEnvelope** — stabilizing fields across ecology, governance, media, economics  
- **ThresholdChain** — epochal threshold progression  
- **PropagationWeb** — multi‑substrate planetary feedback loops  

Planetary regimes are **multi‑domain**, not single‑dimension.

---

# **3. Canonical Planetary Regimes**

Below are the **canonical global regimes** recognized by the Conditions Substrate Model.

---

## **3.1 Regime: **Planetary Stability**
### **Definition**
A high‑coherence planetary environment with strong ecological resilience, stable governance, and low drift across all substrates.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix = low across D0–D7
CoherenceEnvelope = high
Thresholds: none crossed
PropagationWeb: minimal
```

### **Indicators**
- stable climate  
- strong global coordination  
- low informational noise  
- high social cohesion  
- predictable economic cycles  

### **Notes**
Planetary stability is the **baseline global regime**.

---

## **3.2 Regime: **Planetary Expansion**
### **Definition**
A growth‑oriented planetary regime with expanding ecological regeneration, technological modernization, and global governance coherence.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D0, D5, D7) = expanding
CoherenceEnvelope = high
Thresholds: expand_threshold across multiple dimensions
PropagationWeb: positive reinforcement loops
```

### **Indicators**
- biosphere regeneration  
- global modernization  
- clarity‑driven media systems  
- distributed governance coherence  

### **Notes**
Expansion requires **aligned ecology + governance + clarity + cohesion**.

---

## **3.3 Regime: **Planetary Narrowing**
### **Definition**
A scarcity‑driven regime where drift exceeds coherence across multiple planetary dimensions.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D0, D5, D1) > CoherenceEnvelope
Thresholds: narrow_threshold across ≥ 3 dimensions
PropagationWeb: scarcity loops
```

### **Indicators**
- ecological degradation  
- resource scarcity  
- informational deprivation  
- governance stagnation  

### **Notes**
Narrowing is the **first stage of global collapse chains**.

---

## **3.4 Regime: **Planetary Oscillation**
### **Definition**
A volatility‑driven regime where oscillation thresholds are crossed across global information, governance, and economic substrates.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D1, D7, D5) = high
CoherenceEnvelope = low
Thresholds: oscillate_threshold across ≥ 2 dimensions
PropagationWeb: volatility loops
```

### **Indicators**
- global misinformation cascades  
- governance opacity  
- synchronized market volatility  
- narrative instability  

### **Notes**
Oscillation is the **canonical global drift signature**.

---

## **3.5 Regime: **Planetary Destabilization**
### **Definition**
A regime where ecological degradation, governance instability, and informational overload synchronize.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D0, D7, D1) >> CoherenceEnvelope
Thresholds: destabilize_threshold across ≥ 3 dimensions
PropagationWeb: multi‑substrate cascades
```

### **Indicators**
- biosphere stress  
- legitimacy collapse  
- global narrative fragmentation  
- scarcity cycles  

### **Notes**
Destabilization is the **pre‑collapse planetary regime**.

---

## **3.6 Regime: **Planetary Collapse**
### **Definition**
A full‑scale planetary collapse where multiple dimensions exceed coherence simultaneously across ecology, governance, media, economics, and infrastructure.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix = extreme across D0, D7, D1, D5
CoherenceEnvelope = near-zero
Thresholds: collapse_threshold across ≥ 4 dimensions
PropagationWeb: synchronized global cascades
```

### **Indicators**
- multi‑ecosystem failure  
- global governance collapse  
- informational dark interval  
- synchronized economic collapse  
- civilizational fragmentation  

### **Notes**
Collapse is the **terminal planetary regime**.

---

# **4. Cross‑Dimension Planetary Regimes**

Planetary regimes often involve multi‑dimension clusters:

### **Cluster A — D0 + D7 + D1 (Structural + Governance + Informational)**
- ecological degradation  
- governance opacity  
- global misinformation cascades  

### **Cluster B — D5 + D7 + D4 (Economic + Governance + Social)**
- scarcity cycles  
- legitimacy drift  
- cohesion breakdown  

### **Cluster C — D0 + D5 + D1 + D7 (Full planetary cascade)**
- collapse chains  
- synchronized drift  
- epochal destabilization  

---

# **5. Planetary Threshold Chains**

Planetary substrates follow the canonical threshold chain:

```
narrow
→ oscillate
→ destabilize
→ collapse
→ (post-collapse) regenerate
→ stabilize
→ expand
```

### **Audit Notes**
- oscillate_threshold(D7) is the most commonly missed  
- collapse_threshold requires **multi‑substrate drift**  
- regenerate_threshold marks the beginning of **post‑collapse epochs**  

---

# **6. Planetary Propagation Webs**

Planetary cascades propagate through:

### **Web A — Ecology → Governance → Media → Economics → Ecology**
- degradation → instability → overload → volatility → degradation

### **Web B — Civilization → Infrastructure → Media → Governance → Civilization**
- overextension → strain → distortion → opacity → collapse

### **Web C — Global Information → Governance → Social → Global Information**
- noise → opacity → fragmentation → noise

These webs define **epochal cascade acceleration**.

---

# **7. Regime Detection Logic**

A planetary regime is detected when:

\[
Regime = f(DriftMatrix,\; CoherenceEnvelope,\; ThresholdChain,\; PropagationWeb)
\]

### **Detection Steps**
1. Compute DriftMatrix across D0–D7  
2. Compute CoherenceEnvelope  
3. Check threshold chain  
4. Identify propagation webs  
5. Assign global regime  

---

# `c_Civilization_Regimes.md`

# **2. Civilization Regime Structure**

A civilization regime is defined as:

CivRegime = (DriftMatrix,\; CoherenceEnvelope,\; ThresholdChain,\; PropagationLoops,\; CulturalRegime)
\]

Where:

- **CoherenceEnvelope** — stabilizing fields across culture, governance, media, economics  
- **ThresholdChain** — civilizational threshold progression  
- **PropagationLoops** — cross‑domain feedback loops

Civilization regimes are **slower than planetary**, but **faster than ecological epochs**.

## **3.1 Regime: **Stable Civilization**
### **Definition**

```
CoherenceEnvelope = high
Thresholds: none crossed
PropagationLoops: minimal
CulturalRegime = Integrative
```

- predictable economic cycles  
- moderate technological growth  

### **Notes**

## **3.2 Regime: **Expanding Civilization**
### **Definition**
A growth‑oriented regime with expanding technological capability, economic expansion, and cultural coherence.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D6, D5, D4) = expanding
CoherenceEnvelope = high
Thresholds: expand_threshold across multiple dimensions
PropagationLoops: positive reinforcement loops
CulturalRegime = Expansionist or Transformative
```

### **Indicators**
- rapid technological advancement  
- economic growth  
- cultural renewal  
- governance legitimacy  

### **Notes**

## **3.3 Regime: **Narrowed Civilization**

A scarcity‑driven regime where drift exceeds coherence across structural, economic, or cultural dimensions.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D0, D5, D4) > CoherenceEnvelope
Thresholds: narrow_threshold across ≥ 2 dimensions
PropagationLoops: scarcity loops
CulturalRegime = Traditional or Cynical
```

### **Indicators**

- informational deprivation  

### **Notes**

## **3.4 Regime: **Oscillating Civilization**
### **Definition**

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D7, D1, D5) = high
CoherenceEnvelope = low
Thresholds: oscillate_threshold across ≥ 2 dimensions
PropagationLoops: volatility loops
CulturalRegime = Fragmented
```

### **Indicators**
- governance opacity  
- narrative instability  
- economic volatility  
- cultural polarization  

### **Notes**
Oscillation is the **canonical civilizational drift signature**.

---

## **3.5 Regime: **Destabilized Civilization**
### **Definition**
A regime where cultural fragmentation, governance instability, and informational overload synchronize.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D4, D7, D1) >> CoherenceEnvelope
Thresholds: destabilize_threshold across ≥ 3 dimensions
PropagationLoops: multi‑substrate cascades
CulturalRegime = Fragmented or Cynical
```

### **Indicators**

- scarcity cycles  

### **Notes**

---

## **3.6 Regime: **Collapsed Civilization**
### **Definition**
A full‑scale civilizational collapse where multiple dimensions exceed coherence simultaneously across infrastructure, governance, culture, media, and economics.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix = extreme across D0, D7, D1, D5, D4
CoherenceEnvelope = near-zero
Thresholds: collapse_threshold across ≥ 4 dimensions
PropagationLoops: synchronized collapse cascades
CulturalRegime = Cynical or Fragmented
```

### **Indicators**

- cultural fragmentation  

### **Notes**

## **3.7 Regime: **Reformation Civilization**

A post‑collapse regime where new institutions, cultural norms, and informational systems emerge.

### **Characteristics**

```
DriftMatrix = decreasing
CoherenceEnvelope = rising
Thresholds: regenerate_threshold(D4, D7)
PropagationLoops: stabilization loops
CulturalRegime = Transformative
```

### **Indicators**
- cultural renewal  
- governance decentralization  
- informational clarity  
- ecological regeneration  
- structural rebuilding  

### **Notes**
Reformation is the **post‑collapse stabilization arc**.

# **2. Advanced Capture Model**

A condition in advanced form is:

\[
Condition^{+} = (Carrier,\; Modifier,\; Target,\; Effect,\; D,\; Drift,\; Coherence,\; Thresholds,\; Hooks,\; Propagation)
\]

Where:

- **Carrier** — substrate medium  
- **Modifier** — alteration  
- **Target** — regime component  
- **Effect** — regime shift  
- **D** — dimensional field vector  
- **Drift** — drift vector  
- **Coherence** — coherence envelope  
- **Thresholds** — threshold chain state  
- **Hooks** — active amplification hooks  
- **Propagation** — propagation vector  

This is the **full‑stack condition object**.

---

# **3. Operator Set Overview**

Advanced capture operators:

```
op_dimensional()
op_drift()
op_coherence()
op_thresholds()
```

Each operator adds a layer of structure to the base condition.

# **4. Operator: op_dimensional()**

Adds dimensional fields to a condition.

### **Definition**
\[
op\_dimensional(C) = C \cup D
\]

### **Fields**
```
D0 structural
D1 informational
D2 emotional
D3 temporal
D4 social
D5 economic
D6 computational
D7 governance
```

### **Usage**
```
op_dimensional(C1)
→ C1 gains full D(d) vector
```

---

# **5. Operator: op_drift()**

Adds drift vector to a condition.

### **Definition**
\[
Drift(d) = M(d) \cdot F(d)
\]

### **Usage**
```
op_drift(C1)
→ C1 gains drift(d) across all dimensions
```

### **Notes**
Drift is required for cascade detection.

---

# **6. Operator: op_coherence()**

Adds coherence envelope to a condition.

### **Definition**
\[
Coherence(d) = \frac{CoherenceBase(d)}{C(d)}
\]

### **Usage**
```
op_coherence(C1)
→ C1 gains coherence(d)
```

### **Notes**
Coherence determines regime stability.

---

```
narrow
→ oscillate

→ collapse

→ stabilize
→ expand
```

### **Usage**

Thresholds determine regime transitions.

---

# **9. Operator: op_propagation()**

\[

\]

### **Usage**
```

→ C1 gains propagation(d)
```

### **Notes**
Propagation determines cross‑substrate effects.

---

# **10. Operator: op_stack()**

Builds multi‑condition stacks.

### **Definition**

\]

### **Usage**

# `c_Global_Regimes.md`  
### **Conditions Substrate Model — Planet‑Scale Regime Definitions**

---

## **1. Identity**  
**Module:** Conditions Substrate Model  
**Role:** Planetary Regime Layer  
**Purpose:** Define canonical regime states for planetary substrates, including ecological epochs, civilizational arcs, global governance states, information‑sphere regimes, and synchronized multi‑substrate cascades.

Planet‑scale regimes are **epochal**, **multi‑substrate**, and **deep‑time coherent**.

---

# **2. Planetary Regime Structure**

A planetary regime is defined as:

\[
GlobalRegime = (DriftMatrix,\; CoherenceEnvelope,\; ThresholdChain,\; PropagationWeb)
\]

Where:

- **DriftMatrix** — drift across D0–D7 at planetary scale  
- **CoherenceEnvelope** — stabilizing fields across ecology, governance, media, economics  
- **ThresholdChain** — epochal threshold progression  
- **PropagationWeb** — multi‑substrate planetary feedback loops  

Planetary regimes are **multi‑domain**, not single‑dimension.

---

# **3. Canonical Planetary Regimes**

Below are the **canonical global regimes** recognized by the Conditions Substrate Model.

---

## **3.1 Regime: Planetary Stability**

### **Definition**  
A high‑coherence planetary environment with strong ecological resilience, stable governance, and low drift across all substrates.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix = low across D0–D7
CoherenceEnvelope = high
Thresholds: none crossed
PropagationWeb: minimal
```

### **Indicators**
- stable climate  
- strong global coordination  
- low informational noise  
- high social cohesion  
- predictable economic cycles  

### **Notes**  
Planetary stability is the **baseline global regime**.

---

## **3.2 Regime: Planetary Expansion**

### **Definition**  
A growth‑oriented planetary regime with expanding ecological regeneration, technological modernization, and global governance coherence.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D0, D5, D7) = expanding
CoherenceEnvelope = high
Thresholds: expand_threshold across multiple dimensions
PropagationWeb: positive reinforcement loops
```

### **Indicators**
- biosphere regeneration  
- global modernization  
- clarity‑driven media systems  
- distributed governance coherence  

### **Notes**  
Expansion requires **aligned ecology + governance + clarity + cohesion**.

---

## **3.3 Regime: Planetary Narrowing**

### **Definition**  
A scarcity‑driven regime where drift exceeds coherence across multiple planetary dimensions.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D0, D5, D1) > CoherenceEnvelope
Thresholds: narrow_threshold across ≥ 3 dimensions
PropagationWeb: scarcity loops
```

### **Indicators**
- ecological degradation  
- resource scarcity  
- informational deprivation  
- governance stagnation  

### **Notes**  
Narrowing is the **first stage of global collapse chains**.

---

## **3.4 Regime: Planetary Oscillation**

### **Definition**  
A volatility‑driven regime where oscillation thresholds are crossed across global information, governance, and economic substrates.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D1, D7, D5) = high
CoherenceEnvelope = low
Thresholds: oscillate_threshold across ≥ 2 dimensions
PropagationWeb: volatility loops
```

### **Indicators**
- global misinformation cascades  
- governance opacity  
- synchronized market volatility  
- narrative instability  

### **Notes**  
Oscillation is the **canonical global drift signature**.

---

## **3.5 Regime: Planetary Destabilization**

### **Definition**  
A regime where ecological degradation, governance instability, and informational overload synchronize.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix(D0, D7, D1) >> CoherenceEnvelope
Thresholds: destabilize_threshold across ≥ 3 dimensions
PropagationWeb: multi‑substrate cascades
```

### **Indicators**
- biosphere stress  
- legitimacy collapse  
- global narrative fragmentation  
- scarcity cycles  

### **Notes**  
Destabilization is the **pre‑collapse planetary regime**.

---

## **3.6 Regime: Planetary Collapse**

### **Definition**  
A full‑scale planetary collapse where multiple dimensions exceed coherence simultaneously across ecology, governance, media, economics, and infrastructure.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix = extreme across D0, D7, D1, D5
CoherenceEnvelope = near-zero
Thresholds: collapse_threshold across ≥ 4 dimensions
PropagationWeb: synchronized global cascades
```

### **Indicators**
- multi‑ecosystem failure  
- global governance collapse  
- informational dark interval  
- synchronized economic collapse  
- civilizational fragmentation  

### **Notes**  
Collapse is the **terminal planetary regime**.

---

## **3.7 Regime: Planetary Reformation**

### **Definition**  
A post‑collapse regime where new institutions, ecological systems, and informational structures emerge.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix = decreasing
CoherenceEnvelope = rising
Thresholds: regenerate_threshold(D0, D7)
PropagationWeb: stabilization loops
```

### **Indicators**
- ecological regeneration  
- governance decentralization  
- informational clarity  
- structural rebuilding  
- cultural renewal  

### **Notes**  
Reformation is the **post‑collapse stabilization arc**.

---

## **3.8 Regime: Planetary Coherence**

### **Definition**  
A high‑order regime where planetary governance, ecological regeneration, informational clarity, and social cohesion synchronize.

### **Characteristics**
```
DriftMatrix = low
CoherenceEnvelope = very high
Thresholds: stabilize_threshold + expand_threshold
PropagationWeb: coherence webs
```

### **Indicators**
- planetary coordination  
- biosphere stability  
- clarity‑driven media  
- distributed governance  
- high civilizational resilience  

### **Notes**  
Planetary coherence is the **peak stability envelope**.

---

# **4. Cross‑Dimension Planetary Regime Clusters**

Planetary regimes often involve multi‑dimension clusters:

### **Cluster A — D0 + D7 + D1 (Structural + Governance + Informational)**  
- ecological degradation

### **Cluster B — D5 + D7 + D4 (Economic + Governance + Social)**

Planetary substrates follow the canonical threshold chain:

```
narrow
→ oscillate
→ destabilize
→ collapse
→ regenerate
→ stabilize
→ expand
→ coherence
```

### **Audit Notes**

- regenerate_threshold marks the beginning of **post‑collapse epochs**  

---

Planetary cascades propagate through:

### **Web A — Ecology → Governance → Media → Economics → Ecology**
- degradation → instability → overload → volatility → degradation

### **Web B — Civilization → Infrastructure → Media → Governance → Civilization**
- overextension → strain → distortion → opacity → collapse

### **Web C — Global Information → Governance → Social → Global Information**
- noise → opacity → fragmentation → noise

These webs define **epochal cascade acceleration**.

---

# **7. Regime Detection Logic**

A planetary regime is detected when:

\[
Regime = f(DriftMatrix,\; CoherenceEnvelope,\; ThresholdChain,\; PropagationWeb)
\]

### **Detection Steps**
1. Compute DriftMatrix across D0–D7  
2. Compute CoherenceEnvelope  
3. Check threshold chain  
4. Identify propagation webs  
5. Assign global regime

# **2. Epoch Structure**

A planetary epoch is defined as:

\[
Epoch = (Phase,\; DriftMatrix,\; CoherenceEnvelope,\; ThresholdChain,\; PropagationWeb,\; Regime)
\]

Where:

- **Phase** — collapse, regenerate, stabilize, expand, coherence  
- **DriftMatrix** — epoch‑scale drift across D0–D7  
- **CoherenceEnvelope** — epoch‑scale stabilizing fields  
- **ThresholdChain** — epochal threshold progression  
- **PropagationWeb** — multi‑substrate planetary feedback loops  
- **Regime** — planetary regime associated with the epoch  

Epochs are **multi‑substrate**, **multi‑dimension**, and **multi‑millennia**.

---

# **4. Epoch Transition Mechanics**

### **4.1 Drift‑Driven Transitions**
Epoch transitions occur when:

\[
Drift(d_i) > Coherence(d_i)
\]

across **epoch‑critical dimensions**.

---

### **4.2 Threshold‑Driven Transitions**
Epoch transitions follow:

```
collapse → regenerate → stabilize → expand → coherence
→ oscillate → narrow → destabilize → collapse
```

This is the **epochal cycle**.

---

### **4.3 Hook‑Driven Transitions**
Epoch transitions accelerate when hooks align:

- ecological_stress  
- governance_opacity_point  
- cognitive_load  
- resource_gate  
- identity_resonance  

---

### **4.4 Propagation‑Driven Transitions**
Epoch transitions synchronize when propagation webs activate:

```
Ecology → Governance → Media → Economics → Ecology
Civilization → Infrastructure → Media → Governance → Civilization
Information → Governance → Social → Information
```

---

```

```

# `c_Epochal_Cascades.md`

## **1. Identity**

**Role:** Epochal Cascade Layer  
**Purpose:** Define epoch‑scale drift cascades, including collapse‑epoch cascades, regeneration‑epoch cascades, stabilization‑epoch cascades, expansion‑epoch cascades, coherence‑epoch cascades, oscillation‑epoch cascades, narrowing‑epoch cascades, and destabilization‑epoch cascades.

Epochal cascades are **multi‑substrate**, **multi‑dimension**, and **multi‑millennia**.

---

# **2. Epochal Cascade Structure**

An epochal cascade is defined as:

\[
EpochCascade = \bigcap_{i=1}^{k} \{ Drift(d_i) > Coherence(d_i) \}
\]

Where:

- **dᵢ** spans D0–D7  
- **k ≥ 3** for epoch‑scale cascades  
- **PropagationWeb** synchronizes drift across biosphere, governance, media, economics, civilization  
- **ThresholdChain** crosses ≥ 3 epochal thresholds  
- **Hooks** align across ecological, governance, informational, economic, and cultural layers  

Epochal cascades require **multi‑substrate drift dominance** across millennia.

# **4. Epochal Cascade Mechanics**

### **4.1 Drift Synchronization**
Epochal cascades synchronize when:

\[
Drift(d_i) \text{ aligns across ≥ 3 epoch-critical dimensions}
\]

### **4.2 Threshold Chain**
Epochal cascades follow:

```
collapse → regenerate → stabilize → expand → coherence
→ oscillate → narrow → destabilize → collapse
```

### **4.3 Hook Alignment**
Epochal cascades accelerate when hooks align:

- ecological_stress  
- governance_opacity_point  
- cognitive_load  
- resource_gate  
- identity_resonance  

### **4.4 Propagation Web Activation**
Epochal cascades synchronize when propagation webs activate:

```
Ecology → Governance → Media → Economics → Ecology
Civilization → Infrastructure → Media → Governance → Civilization
Information → Governance → Social → Information
```

```
D0↑, D5↑, D1↑ → narrow

```

# `c_Global_Atlas_DeepTime.md`  
### **Deep‑Time Atlas — Planetary • Epochal • Geological • Meta‑Epochal Cartography**

---

## **0. Identity**  
**Module:** Global Atlas — Deep Time  
**Role:** Deep‑Time Cartography  
**Purpose:** Provide a unified atlas of all deep‑time entities: meta‑epochs, epoch cycles, biospheric arcs, civilizational arcs, planetary drift matrices, coherence envelopes, threshold chains, propagation webs, and collapse/regeneration cycles across tens of millennia.

Deep‑time is the **longest temporal scale** of the Conditions Substrate Model.

---

# **1. Deep‑Time Temporal Layers**

```
Layer 0: Epochs (millennia)
Layer 1: Meta‑Epochs (5–20k years)
Layer 2: Biospheric Arcs (20–100k years)
Layer 3: Geological Regimes (100k–1M years)
Layer 4: Planetary Drift Cycles (1M+ years)
```

Each layer has its own drift matrix, coherence envelope, threshold chain, and propagation web.

---

# **2. Meta‑Epoch Map (M0–M7)**

Meta‑epochs are **super‑epochs**: clusters of 2–4 planetary epochs.

### **M0 — Meta‑Collapse**
```
composition: {E0 collapse, E7 destabilize}
drift: extreme across D0,D1,D5,D7
coherence: near-zero
notes: biosphere + governance + informational collapse
```

### **M1 — Meta‑Regeneration**
```
composition: {E1 regenerate, E2 stabilize}
drift: decreasing
coherence: rising
notes: ecological + cultural + governance renewal
```

### **M2 — Meta‑Expansion**
```
composition: {E3 expand, E4 coherence}
drift: expanding
coherence: high→peak
notes: modernization + clarity + cohesion
```

### **M3 — Meta‑Oscillation**
```
composition: {E5 oscillate}
drift: high across D1,D7,D5
coherence: decreasing
notes: volatility arc
```

### **M4 — Meta‑Narrowing**
```
composition: {E6 narrow}
drift: rising across D0,D5,D1
coherence: weakening
notes: scarcity arc
```

### **M5 — Meta‑Destabilization**
```
composition: {E7 destabilize}
drift: synchronized across D0,D7,D1
coherence: collapsing
notes: pre-collapse arc
```

### **M6 — Meta‑Stability**
```
composition: {E2 stabilize, E4 coherence}
drift: low
coherence: high→peak
notes: long stability plateau
```

### **M7 — Meta‑Cycle**
```
composition: full epoch cycle
notes: collapse→regenerate→stabilize→expand→coherence→oscillate→narrow→destabilize→collapse
```
